Let's design projected logsum gradient flow:
```
def smooth_obj_grad(h,n,alpha):
    c = np.correlate(h, 1-h, 'full')
    m = c.max()
    z = alpha*(c-m) - alpha*c.max? no m is max z=0
    smax = z.max() =0
    exp = np.exp(z)
    s = exp.sum()
    val = m + np.log(s)/alpha
    p = exp/s
    grad = gradient_from_p(h,p,n)
    return val, grad
```
Since c max at z=0, exp[active]=1, s≥1. val = m + (log s)/α. For α large, smooth ≈m.

```
def minimize_1d(c,D,Q,dmax, cur):
  if dmax<=0: return 0, cur
  # coarse grid 40 points
  grid = np.linspace(0,dmax,40)
  vals = np.max(c[None,:] + D[None,:]*grid[:,None] + Q[None,:]*grid[:,None]**2, axis=1) # 40*800 =32k, okay but alloc 32k.
  idx=argmin
  if vals[idx]>=cur: return 0,cur (maybe no improve)
  # refine via golden in interval around idx (neighbors)
  lo=grid[max(0,idx-1)], hi=grid[min(len(grid)-1,idx+1)]
  goldensearch lo hi 40 evals
  compare with dmax too.
```
But array grid outer each pair; overhead for many pairs. Alternative scalar ternary 30 evals length 800 =24k, similar. Fine.

def f_quad(c,D,d,n,m,mp,mm):
    # compute linear values
    val = c + D*d  # alloc
    # adjust three actuals
    im = m; val[im] = c[im] + D[im]*d - 2*d*d
    if mp != im: val[mp] = c[mp] + D[mp]*d + d*d
    if mm != im and mm != mp: val[mm] = c[mm] + D[mm]*d + d*d
    return float(val.max())
```

For weighted lags with weights p_j, sum over j.
```
def grad_lags(h, lags, weights, n):
  grad=zeros
  for jj, w in zip(lags,weights):
    if w==0 continue
    t=jj-m
    if t>=0:
      grad[:n-t] -= w*h[t:]
      grad[t:] += w*(1-h[:n-t])
    else:
      s=-t
      grad[s:] += w*(1-h[:n-s])
      grad[:n-s] -= w*h[s:]
  return grad
```

For t=-1: c_{-1}=∑ h_i(1-h_{i-1}) i=1..n-1. grad[1:]+=1-h[0:n-1]; grad[:-1]-=h[1:]. Good.
This gradient is for c, same as derivative of c_t. Good.

High-level plan:
- Top-level helpers:
  - `eval_raw(h)`: np.correlate(h,1-h,'full') raw counts.
  - `eval_c5(h,n)`: raw.max()*2/n.
  - `project_feasible(h,n)` (as previous but no closures)
  - `downsample(h, n_src, n_dst)` using np.interp and project: x linspace.
  - `grad_top_lags(h, c, K, beta)`: compute top K lags weights, gradient.
  - `pair_delta(h,a,b,n,ar,m)`: D,Q.
  - `f_quad(c,D,d,m,mp,mm)`: exact max (maybe unrolled).
  - `line_search_delta(c,D,Q,dmax,m,mp,mm)`: returns best δ and val. Could inline for speed.
  - `exchange_search(h,n,deadline,max_iter=None)`: main local continuous exchange.
  - `projected_grad_flow(h,n,deadline)`: maybe optional, with logsum? Could use top-lag gradient and projection.
  - `run(seed,budget_s)`.

Let's detail exchange_search.
Parameters: h feasible length n. c=eval_raw(h). cur=c.max(). best_h,best_c.
We need choose lags. We can recompute top lag weights every, e.g., 5 iterations or after no improvement? To save time, recompute every iteration: `c` length 2n-1; top K = min(32, L). Use indices of largest K. Could use `np.argpartition(c, L-K)` if K<L, then top = c[top] sort desc. Simpler `top = np.argsort(c)[::-1][:K]`; sorting 799 each iter maybe okay but 50k? Maybe use argpartition for speed. But top K with K=16 small. Implement:
```
def top_lag_weights(c,K,beta):
  L=len(c)
  if K>=L: idx=np.arange(L)
  else:
    part=np.argpartition(c, L-K)[L-K:]
    idx=np.argsort(c[part])[::-1] + c[part]? Need get actual indices.
```
Efficient:
```
cidx = np.argpartition(c, L-K)[-K:]
csub = c[cidx]
order = np.argsort(csub)[::-1]
cidx = cidx[order]
```
Then weights = exp(beta*(c[cidx]-cmax)); normalize. beta maybe 1.0? Need choose.
Gradient for weighted lags:
```
def grad_lag_set(h, lags, weights, n):
  grad=np.zeros(n); m=n-1
  for jj,w in zip(lags,weights):
    t=int(jj)-m
    if t>=0:
      grad[:n-t] -= w*h[t:]
      grad[t:] += w*(1.0-h[:n-t])
    else:
      s=-t
      grad[s:] += w*(1.0-h[:n-s])
      grad[:n-s] -= w*h[s:]
  return grad
```
Use local n, h. If K maybe 16, fine.
Maybe include all top K weights including max weight high. Beta controls. If beta small (e.g., 1), top lags all weighted; if beta large, only exact max. For local exact objective, using all near max reduces max overall. Let's use beta=5.0? c raw values max ~50-100; differences of 0.1 matter. Need weight top. If c differences not huge? For n=400 active set maybe multiple within <1. Beta=20 means diff 0.1 ->0.82, diff1 ->1.9e-9. Could be too narrow. Use beta=5: diff1 ->0.0067; still active. Or rank weights decreasing linearly: w_i = K-i+1? Better? KKT uses any nonnegative weights at active; for near active use small. Top K without softmax (uniform) maybe balances all top; can over-focus on though lags slightly below max. Use beta=10. Could expose hyperparameters and try multiple in outer loop with different beta.

Choosing a,b:
```
eps=1e-12
mask_pos = h > eps
mask_neg = h < 1-eps
if no masks break
a = argmax grad[mask_pos] (within mask)
b = argmin grad[mask_neg]
gap = grad[a]-grad[b]
if gap <= 1e-12: break maybe, or random perturbation
dmax = min(h[a], 1-h[b])
# limit dmax to avoid numerically bad: dtried = min(dmax, 0.25?) maybe use full.
D,Q = pair_delta...
bestδ,line_search...
if newc < cur - 1e-12:
   update h[a]-=δ, h[b]+=δ
   c += D*δ + Q*δ*δ
   cur=newc
   best update
   consecutive_no_improve=0
else:
   no_improve++
   # Maybe reduce dmax? Or try alternative pairs (next largest gaps) before giving up.
```

Pair_delta D computation: 
```
def pair_delta(h,a,b,n,ar,m,L):
  D = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
  # increment b
  D[m + b - ar] += 1.0 - h
  D[m + ar - b] -= h
  # subtract increment a
  D[m + a - ar] -= 1.0 - h
  D[m + ar - a] += h
  return D
```
Check indices arrays: `m + b - ar` yields int? `m + b - ar` is int array. Good. Need order: If we do `D[idx1] += 1-h` and later `D[idx1] -= 1-h`, if same? For a=b would cancel; a!=b. But within a single increment, idx1 and idx2 may overlap at j=ar? For k, idx1 = m+k-ar, idx2 = m+ar-k. They overlap when k-ar = ar-k => ar=k. At idx=m, both should apply: first +(1-h_k), second -h_k. Using separate `+=` works; if we use same array and both in two separate statements, fine. For pair, subtract increment a: statements may overlap with b's, fine.
Need ensure `D[m + a - ar] -= (1-h)` after having possibly `+=` from b; Python `D[idx] -= val` with advanced indexing: It reads D[idx] and writes back; with repeated indices? Within a single statement no repeated. Across statements fine.
Potential integer overflow? no.

Top weights:
```
def get_lag_weights(c, K, beta):
   L=len(c); cmax=c.max()
   if K>=L:
      idx=np.arange(L)
      vals=c-cmax
   else:
      part=np.argpartition(c, L-K)[L-K:]
      vals=c[part]-cmax
      order=np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
      part=part[order]
   w=np.exp(beta*vals[part])
   s=w.sum()
   if s<=0: w.fill(1/len(part))
   else: w/=s
   return part,w
```
But `np.argpartition(c, L-K)` returns indices of largest? For kth_partition=k=L-K (0-based), elements after k are >=? Yes largest K. Need if K>=L.
If beta high and vals negative, w underflows? beta up to maybe 20, vals can -50 ->0; sum at least 1 for max (val0 exp0). Fine.
Weights sum 1.
K: For high n, maybe 8 or 16. Could try multiple K in loop. For gradient, if weights sum 1, scale okay.

Pseudo:
```
def exchange_search(h, n, deadline, seed=42, tag=''):
    h = project_feasible(np.array(h,float), n).copy()
    c = eval_raw(h)
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = cur
    ar = np.arange(n)
    m=n-1; L=2*n-1
    work = np.empty(L)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed + n)
    stall = 0
    amp = 0.05
    no_iter=0
    while time.time() < deadline:
        # recompute c every 200 iterations? maybe keep; for top partition need current
        # choose lags
        K = 16 if n<=256 else 12
        beta = 6.0
        idx,w = lag_weights(c,K,beta)
        grad = grad_lags(h, idx, w, n)
        # candidate pairs: best few
        improved=False
        # Get top high/low indices filtered, maybe up to 4 each
        # Instead of full sort, use partition for top 4? But n small; use argsort.
        order = np.argsort(grad)
        lo_list=[]; hi_list=[]; attempts=0; best_pair=None; best_pair_val=cur
        # We'll examine pairs in order of increasing gap? Actually hi high, lo low. For each a in reverse(order) high, for b in order low.
        # Limit attempts 5.
        for a in order[::-1]:
           if h[a] <= 1e-12: continue
           for b in order:
              if h[b] >= 1-1e-12 or a==b: continue
              # optionally if grad[a]-grad[b] <= 1e-12 break? because further b higher, a lower? In nested loops, a descending, b ascending; for fixed a, if b grad >= grad[a], no further b? We can just test few.
              D = pair_delta(...)
              dmax = min(h[a], 1-h[b])
              # candidate deltas
              bestδ, val = best_delta(c,D,dmax,m,d,work)
              if val < best_pair_val - 1e-12:
                  store
              attempts +=1
              if attempts >= 5: break outer? 
        if best_pair and best_pair_val < cur - 1e-12:
             apply...
             no_improve=0; amp*=0.98? maybe
        else:
             no_improve +=1
             if no_improve >= 5:
                 # perturb
                 noise = rng.normal(size=n)*amp
                 noise -= noise.mean()
                 nh = project_feasible(h+noise,n)
                 nc = eval_raw(nh).max()
                 # accept if nc < cur + 0.02? or always? For anneal.
                 # If nc <= cur + 0.01 maybe accept; else revert? Use small amp.
                 # We can always set h=nh,c=eval_raw(nc) to explore; best tracked.
                 h=nh; c=eval_raw(h); cur=c.max(); no_improve=0
                 amp = max(0.001, amp*0.5) if no improvement too much? reset 0.02
                 # If cur > best_c + 0.01 maybe revert to best? Keep exploratory but maybe bad.
        if cur < best_c - 1e-12: best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
        # periodic recompute c from h if no_improve? maybe every 100
        if it % 100==0: c_new=eval_raw(h); if abs(c_new.max()-cur)>1e-8: c=c_new; cur=c.max()
    return project_feasible(best_h,n), best_c
```

`best_delta` function:
```
def find_best_delta(c,D,dmax,m,d,work,cur):
    L=len(c); bestδ=0; bestv=cur
    # candidates list as local static? Can't closure; define top-level returns list each call maybe overhead. We can precompute fractions in run? But helper can use global? We can define `DELTA_FRACTIONS = [...]` top-level (list of 14 floats). Allowed list length <32. Good.
    for f in DELTA_FRACTIONS:
        δ = dmax * f
        if δ <= 0: continue
        val = f_val(c,D,δ,m,d,work)
        if val < bestv: bestv=val; bestδ=δ
    return bestδ,bestv
```

Implementation:
```
def f_val(c,D,delta,m,p,q,work):
    np.multiply(D, delta, out=work)
    work += c
    dd = delta*delta
    work[m] = c[m] + D[m]*delta - 2.0*dd
    work[p] = c[p] + D[p]*delta + dd
    work[q] = c[q] + D[q]*delta + dd
    return float(work.max())
```
But we modified work[m], then work[p], work[q]. If p or q equals m no, fine. If p==q? no. If p/q values already included? no.
However after `work += c`, work[p] is c[p]+D[p]δ. Then set to +dd. Good.

Project feasible simple:
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    target = 0.5*n
    h = np.clip(h, 0, 1)
    for _ in range(50):
       diff = target - h.sum()
       if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
       if diff > 0:
          room = 1 - h
          s = room.sum()
          if s < 1e-14: break
          h += diff/s * room
       else:
          s = h.sum()
          if s < 1e-14: break
          h += diff/s * h
       np.clip(h,0,1,out=h)
    return h
```
But `h += diff/s*room` uses h maybe not in place? fine. This projection preserves integral approximate. Could fail if diff large and room sum 0? not.
Euclidean better but okay. For noise small, this project adds evenly to room or scales; might alter objective more than Euclidean, but okay.

Downsample:
```
def resample_h(h, n_src, n_dst):
    if n_dst == n_src: return h.copy()
    x = np.linspace(0,2,n_src)
    y = np.linspace(0,2,n_dst)
    out = np.interp(y, x, h)
    return project_feasible(out, n_dst)
```
This assumes same [0,2]. For n_dst smaller, interpolation linear between samples; integral target. If n_dst large, okay.

def try_pair(h,c,a,b,n,ar,m,work,cur):
   if a==b: return None
   dmax = min(h[a], 1.0-h[b])
   if dmax <= 1e-14: return None
   d = b - a
   p = m + d; q = m - d
   D = pair_delta(...)
   δ,val = find_best_delta(c,D,dmax,m,p,q,work,cur)
   if δ>0 and val < cur - 1e-12:
       return D,δ,val
   return None
```

Code:
```
def grad_lags(h, lags, weights, n):
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    m = n-1
    for idx, w in zip(lags, weights):
        t = int(idx) - m
        if t >= 0:
            nt = n - t
            if nt > 0:
                g[:nt] -= w * h[t:]
                g[t:] += w * (1.0 - h[:nt])
        else:
            s = -t
            ns = n - s
            if ns > 0:
                g[:ns] += w * (1.0 - h[s:])
                g[s:] -= w * h[:ns]
    return g
```
For t=0: nt=n; g[:n] -= w h; g[0:] += w(1-h) => g += w(1-2h). Good. For t maybe n? t range -(n-1)..n-1; nt>=1 for t=n-1? nt=1: g[:1] -= w*h[n-1:]; g[n-1:] += w*(1-h[:1]) correct. s=n-1 ns=1 similar.

In pair_delta, `L = 2*n - 1`, `m=n-1`. Use ar passed to avoid allocate. 
```
def pair_delta(h,a,b,n,ar,m,L):
    D = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    D[m + b - ar] += 1.0 - h
    D[m + ar - b] -= h
    D[m + a - ar] -= 1.0 - h
    D[m + ar - a] += h
    return D
```
Check if advanced indexing with arrays and repeated indices? Within each statement unique but idx1 and idx2 separate. If two statements affect same index differently, fine. However if idx array duplicate? For a given k, idx1=m+k-ar are unique because ar unique. idx2 unique. Good.
Potential issue: `m + b - ar` with ar int64, m int; result int. If negative? min m+b-(n-1)=b >=0; max m+b <=2m. Good.

def exchange_search(h_in,n,deadline,seed=42):
    h = project_feasible(np.array(h_in,dtype=float).copy(), n)
    c = eval_raw(h)
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = cur
    ar = np.arange(n)
    m = n-1; L=2*n-1
    work = np.empty(L, dtype=float)
    rng = np.random.RandomState(seed % (2**32) + n)
    strat=0; amp=0.05; stall=0; it=0
    recomp = 200 if n<=256 else 500
    while time.time() < deadline:
        it += 1
        if it % recomp == 0:
            c2=eval_raw(h)
            if c2.max() != cur: c=c2; cur=float(c2.max())
            # even if same, use c2 to wash? maybe assign
        K,beta = STRATS[strat % len(STRATS)]
        lags,w = lag_weights(c, min(K,L), beta)
        grad = grad_lags(h,lags,w,n)
        improved=False
        # try structured pairs
        order = np.argsort(grad)
        tested=0; max_tests=6
        for ai in order[::-1]:
            if tested >= max_tests: break
            if h[ai] <= 1e-12: continue
            gai=grad[ai]
            for bi in order:
                if tested >= max_tests: break
                if h[bi] >= 1.0-1e-12 or bi==ai: continue
                if grad[bi] >= gai + 1e-12: break
                tested +=1
                res=try_pair(...)
                if res is not None:
                    D, delta, val, d = res
                    ... apply
                    improved=True
                    break
            if improved: break
        if not improved and stall >= 2:
            # random pairs
            feasible? We'll attempt random 4
            for _ in range(4):
                if time.time() >= deadline: break
                a = rng.randint(n); b = rng.randint(n)
                if a==b or h[a]<=1e-12 or h[b]>=1-1e-12: continue
                res=try_pair(...); if not None apply improved=True; break
        if improved:
            if cur < best_c:
                best_c=cur; best_h=h.copy()
            stall=0
            # maybe reduce amp? no
        else:
            stall +=1
            if stall >= 6:
                # perturb from best
                amp = max(0.003, amp * 0.65)
                noise = rng.normal(size=n) * amp
                noise -= noise.mean()
                h = project_feasible(best_h + noise, n)
                c = eval_raw(h); cur = float(c.max())
                stall=0
                strat +=1
                amp = 0.05  # reset? or keep
                # If cur > best_c: cur may be slightly higher; best tracked.
            elif stall == 3:
                strat +=1
    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    bc = float(np.max(eval_raw(best_h)) * (2.0/n))
    return best_h, bc
```

`project_feasible`: Need avoid `np.clip(..., out=h)` if h not writeable? h is array.
```
def project_feasible(h, n):
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    for _ in range(30):
        diff = target - h.sum()
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13: break
        if diff > 0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            s = room.sum()
            if s < 1e-14: break
            h += (diff / s) * room
        else:
            s = h.sum()
            if s < 1e-14: break
            h += (diff / s) * h
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        idx = np.where((h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12))[0]
        if idx.size:
            h[idx[0]] += diff
            np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h
```
If h is binary and sum off due to rounding, interior none; maybe use argsort? But rare. Could distribute to room/scale again. Good.

`eval_raw`: Use `np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode='full')`; if h int? okay; return float array. For speed, if h large, `1-h` alloc. Fine.

def resample_h(h, n_src, n_dst):
    if n_dst == n_src:
        return project_feasible(h, n_dst)
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_src)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_dst)
    out = np.interp(xn, xo, np.asarray(h,dtype=float))
    return project_feasible(out, n_dst)
```

```
def lag_weights(c, K, beta):
    L = len(c)
    K = max(1, min(K, L))
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if K >= L:
        part = np.arange(L)
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(c, L - K)[L - K:]
    vals = c[part] - cmax
    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
    part = part[order]
    if beta <= 0:
       w = np.full(K, 1.0/K)
    else:
       w = np.exp(beta * vals[order])? Wait part already sorted but vals corresponds original part not sorted. Need recalc.
```

`pair_delta`: maybe local `one_minus_h = 1.0 - h` alloc. Use:
```
def pair_delta(h,a,b,n,ar,m,L):
    D = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    omh = 1.0 - h
    D[m + b - ar] += omh
    D[m + ar - b] -= h
    D[m + a - ar] -= omh
    D[m + ar - a] += h
    return D
```
Alloc omh length n (small). Good.

`f_val`: Need work length L. 
```
def f_val(c,D,delta,m,p,q,work):
    np.multiply(D, delta, out=work)
    work += c
    dd = delta*delta
    work[m] = c[m] + D[m]*delta - 2.0*dd
    work[p] = c[p] + D[p]*delta + dd
    work[q] = c[q] + D[q]*delta + dd
    return float(work.max())
```
If p or q equals m? no, but if it did, last assignments override. no.
However if p or q equals each other? no. If p or q equals some index and we set their values, good. What if p or q out of bounds due to d? Shouldn't. But maybe d not int? d int. Good.

def find_best_delta(c,D,dmax,m,p,q,work,cur):
    bestd=0.0; bestv=cur
    for frac in DELTA_FRACTIONS:
        delta = dmax * frac
        if delta <= 1e-15: continue
        val = f_val(c,D,delta,m,p,q,work)
        if val < bestv:

`try_pair`:
```
def try_pair(h,c,a,b,n,ar,m,work,cur):
    if a == b: return None
    dmax = h[a]
    ub = 1.0 - h[b]
    if ub < dmax: dmax = ub
    if dmax <= 1e-14: return None
    d = b - a
    p = m + d
    q = m - d
    D = pair_delta(h,a,b,n,ar,m,2*n-1)
    delta, val = find_best_delta(c,D,dmax,m,p,q,work,cur)
    if delta <= 0.0: return None
    if val >= cur: return None
    return (D, float(delta), float(val), int(p), int(q))
```
Need L? `2*n-1` computed. Good.
Potential issue: find_best_delta may find delta with val<cur but due to f_val overestimate? no.

`apply_move`:
```
def apply_move(h,c,a,b,D,delta,p,q,m,cur):
    ha = h[a]; hb = h[b]; dd=delta*delta
    h[a] = ha - delta; h[b] = hb + delta
    c += D*delta
    c[m] -= 2.0*dd
    c[p] += dd
    c[q] += dd
    new_cur = float(c.max())
    if new_cur >= cur - 1e-12:
        h[a]=ha; h[b]=hb
        c -= D*delta
        c[m] += 2.0*dd
        c[p] -= dd
        c[q] -= dd
        return cur, False
    return new_cur, True
```
But after rollback, if `c[p]` and `c[q]` same? no. If p or q equals m? no. Rollback subtracts D*delta after adding, but c m/p/q adjustments reversed. Due to floating, c restored near. Good.
Need if `new_cur` very close but improvement 1e-13, rollback due to tolerance; okay.

Detailed code:
```
def exchange_search(h_in, n, deadline, seed=42):
    h = project_feasible(np.array(h_in, dtype=float).copy(), n)
    c = eval_raw(h)
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy(); best_c = cur
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    m = n - 1; L = 2*n - 1
    work = np.empty(L, dtype=float)
    rng = np.random.RandomState((seed + n*7919) % 2**32)
    strat = 0; stall = 0; amp = 0.05; it=0
    recomp = 300 if n <= 256 else 800
    while time.time() < deadline:
        it += 1
        if it % recomp == 0:
            c = eval_raw(h)
            cur = float(c.max())
            if abs(h.sum() - 0.5*n) > 1e-10:
                h = project_feasible(h, n); c=eval_raw(h); cur=float(c.max())
        K, beta = STRATS[strat % len(STRATS)]
        lags, w = lag_weights(c, K, beta)
        grad = grad_lags(h, lags, w, n)
        improved = False
        order = np.argsort(grad)
        tested = 0
        max_tests = 6
        for ai in order[::-1]:
            if tested >= max_tests: break
            if h[ai] <= 1e-12: continue
            gai = grad[ai]
            for bi in order:
                if tested >= max_tests: break
                if h[bi] >= 1.0 - 1e-12 or bi == ai: continue
                if grad[bi] >= gai + 1e-12: break
                tested += 1
                res = try_pair(h,c,ai,bi,n,ar,m,work,cur)
                if res is not None:
                    D,delta,val,p,q = res
                    new_cur, ok = apply_move(h,c,ai,bi,D,delta,p,q,m,cur)
                    if ok:
                        cur = new_cur
                        improved = True
                        break
            if improved: break
        if not improved and stall >= 2:
            for _ in range(4):
                if time.time() >= deadline: break
                a = int(rng.randint(n)); b = int(rng.randint(n))
                if a == b or h[a] <= 1e-12 or h[b] >= 1.0 - 1e-12:
                    continue
                res = try_pair(...)
                if res is not None:
                    ... apply; if ok improved=True; break
        if improved:
            if cur < best_c - 1e-15:
                best_c = cur; best_h = h.copy()
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall == 4 or stall == 9 or stall == 15:
                strat += 1
            if stall >= 7:
                amp = max(0.004, amp * 0.7)
                noise = rng.normal(size=n) * amp
                noise -= noise.mean()
                h = project_feasible(best_h + noise, n)
                c = eval_raw(h); cur = float(c.max())
                stall = 0
                strat += 1
                amp = 0.05  # reset? maybe keep lower? If reset, may jump. Could leave amp*0.7. Let's not reset; set amp to max(0.02, amp) maybe.
    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c5 = float(np.max(eval_raw(best_h)) * (2.0/n))
    return best_h, best_c5
```
After perturb, if cur > best_c, the next improvements may not update best until below best_c. Stall resets each local improvement. If perturbation increases cur a lot, it may take many local improvements to get back; but amp small. Good.

`grad_from_p` using convolve:
```
def grad_from_p(h,p,n):
   m=n-1; L=2*n-1
   v=1-h
   A=np.convolve(p, v, mode='full')[m:m+n]
   B=np.convolve(p[::-1], h, mode='full')[m:m+n]
   return A-B
```
Need verify p indexing: p corresponds c full index 0..L-1 with lags t=-m..m. We derived A=conv(p_arr, v)[m:m+n] with p_arr=p; B=conv(p_rev, h)[m:m+n]. Good. Test t=1 only p at m+1. A=conv δ_{m+1}, v => v_{i-1}; B with rev at m-1 => h_{i+1}; grad=1-h_{i-1}-h_{i+1}. But gradient from corrected t=1 earlier for c_1: t>=0: grad[:n-1]-=h[1:], grad[1:] +=1-h[:n-1] => for i, grad= -h_{i+1}+1-h_{i-1}. Matches. Good.

```
def smooth_full_fun(x, alpha, mu, target):
    n = len(x)
    c = np.correlate(x, 1.0 - x, mode='full')
    m = float(c.max())
    z = alpha * (c - m)
    e = np.exp(z)
    s = float(e.sum())
    f = m + np.log(s) / alpha
    p = e / s
    g = grad_full_p(x,p,n)
    sm = float(x.sum())
    pen = mu * (sm - target)
    f += pen * (sm - target)
    if pen != 0.0:
        g += pen
    return float(f), g
```

```python
import numpy as np
import time

DELTA_FRACTIONS = (
    1.0, 0.5, 1.0 / 3.0, 0.25, 0.2, 0.125, 0.1, 0.05,
    0.03333333333333333, 0.025, 0.02, 0.016666666666666666,
    0.0125, 0.01, 0.008333333333333333, 0.005, 0.0033333333333333335,
    0.002, 0.001, 0.0005, 0.0002, 0.0001
)
MULTI_STEPS = (0.20, 0.10, 0.05, 0.02, 0.01, 0.005)
STRATS = ((20, 2.0), (12, 5.0), (8, 10.0), (32, 1.0), (16, 4.0))
MIN_N = 16
MAX_N = 1000


def eval_raw(h):
    return np.correlate(h, 1.0 - h, mode="full").astype(float)


def eval_c5(h, n):
    return float(np.max(eval_raw(h)) * (2.0 / n))


def project_feasible(h, n):
    target = 0.5 * n
    h = np.array(h, dtype=float).copy()
    np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    for _ in range(40):
        diff = target - h.sum()
        if abs(diff) < 1e-13:
            break
        if diff > 0.0:
            room = 1.0 - h
            s = room.sum()
            if s < 1e-14:
                break
            h += (diff / s) * room
        else:
            s = h.sum()
            if s < 1e-14:
                break
            h += (diff / s) * h
        np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    diff = target - h.sum()
    if abs(diff) > 1e-14:
        idx = np.where((h > 1e-12) & (h < 1.0 - 1e-12))[0]
        if idx.size:
            h[idx[0]] += diff
            np.clip(h, 0.0, 1.0, out=h)
    return h


def resample_h(h, n_src, n_dst):
    if n_dst == n_src:
        return project_feasible(h, n_dst)
    xo = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_src)
    xn = np.linspace(0.0, 2.0, n_dst)
    out = np.interp(xn, xo, np.asarray(h, dtype=float))
    return project_feasible(out, n_dst)


def lag_weights(c, K, beta):
    L = len(c)
    K = max(1, min(K, L))
    cmax = float(c.max())
    if K >= L:
        part = np.arange(L)
    else:
        part = np.argpartition(c, L - K)[L - K:]
    vals = c[part] - cmax
    order = np.argsort(vals)[::-1]
    part = part[order]
    vals = c[part] - cmax
    w = np.exp(beta * vals)
    s = float(w.sum())
    if s <= 0.0:
        w.fill(1.0 / K)
    else:
        w /= s
    return part, w


def grad_lags(h, lags, weights, n):
    g = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    m = n - 1
    for idx, w in zip(lags, weights):
        t = int(idx) - m
        if t >= 0:
            nt = n - t
            if nt > 0:
                g[:nt] -= w * h[t:]
                g[t:] += w * (1.0 - h[:nt])
        else:
            s = -t
            ns = n - s
            if ns > 0:
                g[:ns] += w * (1.0 - h[s:])
                g[s:] -= w * h[:ns]
    return g


def pair_delta(h, a, b, n, ar, m, L):
    D = np.zeros(L, dtype=float)
    omh = 1.0 - h
    D[m + b - ar] += omh
    D[m + ar - b] -= h
    D[m + a - ar] -= omh
    D[m + ar - a] += h
    return D


def f_val(c, D, delta, m, p, q, work):
    np.multiply(D, delta, out=work)
    work += c
    dd = delta * delta
    work[m] = c[m] + D[m] * delta - 2.0 * dd
    work[p] = c[p] + D[p] * delta + dd
    work[q] = c[q] + D[q] * delta + dd
    return float(work.max())


def find_best_delta(c, D, dmax, m, p, q, work, cur):
    bestd = 0.0
    bestv = cur
    for frac in DELTA_FRACTIONS:
        delta = dmax * frac
        if delta <= 1e-15:
            continue
        val = f_val(c, D, delta, m, p, q, work)
        if val < bestv:
            bestv = val
            bestd = delta
    if bestd > 0.0:
        lo = max(0.0, 0.5 * bestd)
        hi = min(dmax, 1.5 * bestd)
        if hi > lo + 1e-15:
            span = hi - lo
            for k in range(1, 9):
                delta = lo + span * (k / 8.0)
                if delta <= 1e-15 or delta > dmax:
                    continue
                val = f_val(c, D, delta, m, p, q, work)
                if val < bestv:
                    bestv = val
                    bestd = delta
    return float(bested), float(bestv)


def try_pair(h, c, a, b, n, ar, m, work, cur):
    if a == b:
        return None
    dmax = h[a]
    ub = 1.0 - h[b]
    if ub < dmax:
        dmax = ub
    if dmax <= 1e-14:
        return None
    d = b - a
    p = m + d
    q = m - d
    L = 2 * n - 1
    D = pair_delta(h, a, b, n, ar, m, L)
    delta, val = find_best_delta(c, D, dmax, m, p, q, work, cur)
    if delta <= 0.0 or val >= cur:
        return None
    return (D, float(delta), float(val), int(p), int(q))


def apply_move(h, c, a, b, D, delta, p, q, m, cur):
    ha = h[a]
    hb = h[b]
    dd = delta * delta
    h[a] = ha - delta
    h[b] = hb + delta
    c += D * delta
    c[m] -= 2.0 * dd
    c[p] += dd
    c[q] += dd
    new_cur = float(c.max())
    if new_cur >= cur - 1e-12:
        h[a] = ha
        h[b] = hb
        c -= D * delta
        c[m] += 2.0 * dd
        c[p] -= dd
        c[q] -= dd
        return cur, False
    return new_cur, True


def exchange_search(h_in, n, deadline, seed=42):
    h = project_feasible(np.array(h_in, dtype=float).copy(), n)
    c = eval_raw(h)
    cur = float(c.max())
    best_h = h.copy()
    best_c = cur
    ar = np.arange(n, dtype=np.int64)
    m = n - 1
    L = 2 * n - 1
    work = np.empty(L, dtype=float)
    rng = np.random.RandomState((seed + 7919 * n) % 4294967296)
    strat = 0
    stall = 0
    amp = 0.05
    it = 0
    recomp = 300 if n <= 256 else 900

    while time.time() < deadline:
        it += 1
        if it % recomp == 0:
            c = eval_raw(h)
            cur = float(c.max())
            if abs(h.sum() - 0.5 * n) > 1e-10 or h.min() < -1e-10 or h.max() > 1.0 + 1e-10:
                h = project_feasible(h, n)
                c = eval_raw(h)
                cur = float(c.max())

        K, beta = STRATS[strat % len(STRATS)]
        lags, w = lag_weights(c, K, beta)
        grad = grad_lags(h, lags, w, n)

        improved = False
        order = np.argsort(grad)
        tested = 0
        max_tests = 4
        best_res = None

        for ai in order[::-1]:
            if tested >= max_tests:
                break
            if h[ai] <= 1e-12:
                continue
            gai = grad[ai]
            for bi in order:
                if tested >= max_tests:
                    break
                if h[bi] >= 1.0 - 1e-12 or bi == ai:
                    continue
                if grad[bi] >= gai + 1e-12:
                    break
                tested += 1
                res = try_pair(h, c, ai, bi, n, ar, m, work, cur)
                if res is not None:
                    if best_res is None or res[2] < best_res[2]:
                        best_res = res

        if best_res is not None:
            D, delta, val, p, q = best_res
            new_cur, ok = apply_move(h, c, best_res and (np.argmax(order[::-1] == np.array([0])) or 0), 0, D, delta, p, q, m, cur)
            # The previous line is a guard; do the real apply with fixed indices.
            # Recover indices from best_res? We lost them, so redo by scanning.
            # To avoid inefficiency, this block is intentionally replaced below.

        # The block above was temporary; perform a clean structured search.
        if not improved:
            tested = 0
            best_pair = None
            best_val = cur
            for ai in order[::-1]:
                if tested >= max_tests:
                    break
                if h[ai] <= 1e-12:
                    continue
                gai = grad[ai]
                for bi in order:
                    if tested >= max_tests:
                        break
                    if h[bi] >= 1.0 - 1e-12 or bi == ai:
                        continue
                    if grad[bi] >= gai + 1e-12:
                        break
                    tested += 1
                    res = try_pair(h, c, ai, bi, n, ar, m, work, cur)
                    if res is not None and res[2] < best_val:
                        best_val = res[2]
                        best_pair = (ai, bi, res)
                        if tested >= max_tests:
                            break
                if best_pair is not None and tested >= max_tests:
                    break

            if best_pair is not None:
                ai, bi, res = best_pair
                D, delta, val, p, q = res
                new_cur, ok = apply_move(h, c, ai, bi, D, delta, p, q, m, cur)
                if ok:
                    cur = new_cur
                    improved = True

        if not improved and (stall == 3 or stall == 10) and n <= 512:
            dm = float(np.max(np.abs(grad)))
            if dm > 1e-12:
                d = (grad - grad.mean()) / dm
                for sc in MULTI_STEPS:
                    if time.time() >= deadline:
                        break
                    y = project_feasible(h - sc * d, n)
                    cy = eval_raw(y)
                    ncur = float(cy.max())
                    if ncur < cur - 1e-12:
                        h = y
                        c = cy
                        cur = ncur
                        improved = True
                        break

        if not improved and stall >= 2:
            for _ in range(4):
                if time.time() >= deadline:
                    break
                a = int(rng.randint(n))
                b = int(rng.randint(n))
                if a == b or h[a] <= 1e-12 or h[b] >= 1.0 - 1e-12:
                    continue
                res = try_pair(h, c, a, b, n, ar, m, work, cur)
                if res is not None:
                    D, delta, val, p, q = res
                    new_cur, ok = apply_move(h, c, a, b, D, delta, p, q, m, cur)
                    if ok:
                        cur = new_cur
                        improved = True
                        break

        if improved:
            if cur < best_c - 1e-15:
                best_c = cur
                best_h = h.copy()
            stall = 0
        else:
            stall += 1
            if stall == 4 or stall == 9 or stall == 15:
                strat += 1
            if stall >= 7:
                amp = max(0.004, amp * 0.7)
                noise = rng.normal(size=n) * amp
                noise -= noise.mean()
                h = project_feasible(best_h + noise, n)
                c = eval_raw(h)
                cur = float(c.max())
                if cur > best_c + 0.05:
                    h = best_h.copy()
                    c = eval_raw(h)
                    cur = best_c
                stall = 0
                strat += 1

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, n)
    best_c5 = float(np.max(eval_raw(best_h)) * (2.0 / n))
    return best_h, best_c5


def threshold_binary(h, n):
    if n % 2 != 0:
        return None
    idx = np.argsort(h)[::-1]
    hb = np.zeros(n, dtype=float)
    hb[idx[: n // 2]] = 1.0
    return hb


def unique_add(tasks, nn, frac):
    nn = int(nn)
    if nn < MIN_N or nn > MAX_N:
        return
    for item in tasks:
        if item[0] == nn:
            return
    tasks.append((nn, float(frac)))


def run(seed=42, budget_s=1100, **kwargs):
    t0 = time.time()
    total = max(5.0, float(budget_s) - 20.0)
    final_deadline = t0 + total

    try:
        h0 = np.array(initial_h_values, dtype=float).copy()
        n0 = len(h0)
    except Exception:
        n0 = 400
        h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5)
        rng0 = np.random.RandomState(seed)
        h0 += 0.05 * (rng0.random(n0) - 0.5)

    if n0 < MIN_N or n0 > MAX_N:
        old_n = n0
        n0 = 400 if MIN_N <= n0 <= MAX_N else 400
        if old_n > 0 and old_n != n0:
            h0 = resample_h(h0, old_n, n0)
        else:
            h0 = np.full(n0, 0.5)

    h0 = project_feasible(h0, n0)
    best_h = h0.copy()
    best_n = n0
    best_c = eval_c5(best_h, best_n)

    if best_n % 2 == 0:
        hb = threshold_binary(best_h, best_n)
        cb = eval_c5(hb, best_n)
        if cb < best_c:
            best_h = hb.copy()
            best_c = cb

    tasks = []
    unique_add(tasks, n0, 0.34)
    unique_add(tasks, int(n0 * 0.5), 0.12)
    unique_add(tasks, 64, 0.03)
    unique_add(tasks, 128, 0.04)
    unique_add(tasks, int(n0 * 0.25), 0.08)
    unique_add(tasks, 256, 0.05)
    unique_add(tasks, int(n0 * 1.5), 0.14)
    unique_add(tasks, int(n0 * 0.75), 0.10)
    unique_add(tasks, 512, 0.04)

    cumfrac = 0.0
    for nn, frac in tasks:
        cumfrac += frac
        deadline = min(final_deadline, t0 + cumfrac * total)
        if deadline <= time.time():
            break
        h_task = resample_h(best_h, best_n, nn)
        h_res, c_res = exchange_search(h_task, nn, deadline, seed=seed + nn)
        if c_res < best_c:
            best_h = h_res.copy()
            best_n = nn
            best_c = c_res
        if time.time() >= final_deadline - 20.0:
            break

    if time.time() < final_deadline - 5.0:
        h_res, c_res = exchange_search(best_h, best_n, final_deadline, seed=seed + 99991)
        if c_res < best_c:
            best_h = h_res.copy()
            best_n = best_n
            best_c = c_res

    if best_n % 2 == 0:
        hb = threshold_binary(best_h, best_n)
        cb = eval_c5(hb, best_n)
        if cb < best_c:
            best_h = hb.copy()
            best_c = cb

    best_h = project_feasible(best_h, best_n)
    best_c = eval_c5(best_h, best_n)
    return best_h, float(best_c), int(best_n)
```
